DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
Control a luminous dot that constantly leaves a delayed echo of its path. By tapping, you instantly swap the dot with its echo, letting you slip past obstacles that target only one of the two.

## Player
The avatar is a bright moving dot with a translucent trailing echo. Verbs: move (steer the dot continuously), tap (swap positions with the echo).

## Core loop
Each frame the dot moves under player control while the echo faithfully follows the same trajectory with a short lag. Obstacles appear, some affecting only the dot, others only the echo. The player judges the right moment to tap, swapping positions, then continues guiding the dot as the echo resumes its delayed trail. The cycle repeats, with new hazards and opportunities emerging constantly.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Solid spikes** that instantly end the game if the dot touches them.
- **Moving barriers** that sweep across the play area and only collide with the dot.
- **Ghost walls** that are harmless to the dot but destroy the echo on contact, forcing the player to avoid swapping at the wrong time.
- **Laser sweeps** that trigger on the dot’s current location but ignore the echo’s delayed position, rewarding precise timing.

## Score
Score increases each time the player successfully swaps to avoid a hazard, each time a swap is performed, and each time a collectible orb is passed while moving.

## Win and lose
The player loses when the dot contacts a solid spike, a moving barrier, or any hazard that kills it. The player wins by reaching a designated exit zone after surviving a set number of swaps (or a time limit) and triggering a final “escape” swap. Upon win or loss a bold overlay appears showing the result and the final score.

## Feel
A fast‑paced, reflex‑driven experience with a neon‑glow aesthetic; the background pulses with subtle gradients while the dot and its echo emit bright cyan‑magenta trails. Minimalistic synth chimes signal swaps and hazard interactions, creating a sleek, arcade‑like atmosphere.

## Why it's fun
Swapping places with your own delayed echo lets you magically “step through” walls, delivering a satisfying moment of surprise and mastery whenever a tight timing swap lets you survive an otherwise impossible obstacle.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold + meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, description, controls list, canvas (800×600), restart button, empty `<script>` block, and create `meta.json` matching the header text and controls.
2. Game state objects — define player object (x:400, y:300, vx:0, vy:0, radius:10), hazards array, score variable, phase string, constants `SAFE_RADIUS=200`, `MAX_SPEED=300` (px/s), and other needed values at top of script.
3. requestAnimationFrame loop — implement `startLoop()` with `cancelAnimationFrame` discipline, `loopId` tracking, `dt` calculation (clamp to 0.1 s), `try/catch` wrapper, calling `update(dt)` and `render()` each frame, and initial `requestAnimationFrame(startLoop)`.
4. Input handling — add a `keys` Set, `keydown`/`keyup` listeners for arrow keys (movement) and `R` (restart), plus click listener on `#restart` button; both call a common `reset()` function.
5. Player movement — in `update(dt)` apply acceleration (600 px/s²) when arrow keys are held, apply friction (0.9 factor), cap speed at `MAX_SPEED`, update position with `dt`, and clamp to canvas bounds.
6. Hazard spawning — implement spawn timer (e.g., every 1.0 s) that creates hazard objects with random positions outside `SAFE_RADIUS` of player and ensures they cannot reach the player within 3 s at speed `200 px/s`; add to hazards array.
7. Hazard motion — each frame move hazards according to their velocity (scaled by `dt`), using a simple linear motion pattern.
8. Collision detection — detect when distance between player and any hazard < sum of radii; on hit set `phase="gameover"`, stop the active gameplay loop.
9. Score handling — increase `score` on discrete events (e.g., each hazard that passes the player without collision), and render the numeric score in the top‑left corner of the canvas.
10. Rendering — clear canvas, draw background, draw hazards, draw player (high‑contrast neon), and draw HUD elements (score, etc.). Ensure player is the brightest object.
11. Game‑over overlay — dim the background, draw "Game Over", final score, and "Press R or click Restart" text on the canvas.
12. Observable contract — each frame assign `window.__game = { state: phase, score, player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: true }, objective: "Swap the dot with its delayed echo to bypass obstacles." }`.
13. Audio — lazily create an `AudioContext` in a `beep()` helper, wrap calls in `try/catch`, and play distinct tones on hit, score increment, and death events.

  </style>

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Echo Swap</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Control a moving dot that leaves a delayed echo of its path; tap to swap positions with the echo and bypass obstacles.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or WASD to move</li>
      <li>Space or click to swap dot and echo</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const SAFE_RADIUS = 200;
    const MAX_SPEED = 300; // px/s

    // === STATE ==============================================================
    let player = { x: 400, y: 300, vx: 0, vy: 0, radius: 10 };
    let hazards = [];
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu"; // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"
    // Additional state placeholders
    let keys = new Set();
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    let loopId = null;
    let audioCtx = null;
    const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // Placeholder reset: reinitialize state
      player = { x: 400, y: 300, vx: 0, vy: 0, radius: 10 };
      hazards = [];
      score = 0;
      phase = "menu";
      keys.clear();
      lastTime = performance.now();
      startLoop();
    }

    // === LOOP / UPDATE ======================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) { console.error("loop error", err); }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // === UPDATE ==============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      // Placeholder update logic
    }

    // === RENDER ==============================================================
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Draw placeholder player
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // === BOOT ===============================================================
    reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

## Concept:
One or two sentences in own words: The player controls a moving dot that leaves a trailing echo with a short delay; tapping swaps the dot with its echo, allowing it to bypass obstacles that affect only one of them.

## Player:
Describe avatar: A single luminous dot; its echo is a ghosted counterpart. Verbs: move (continuous direction with input), tap (swap positions with echo). Possibly also pause? But main is move and tap.

## Core loop:
In each moment, the player guides the dot across a field, watching the delayed echo trace the path. Obstacles appear; the player decides when to swap positions. After a swap, the dot inherits the echo's location, the echo restarts trailing, etc. The core loop repeats.

Perhaps more detailed: The player continuously moves the dot (by mouse or arrow keys) while the echo follows the exact path with a short lag. The player must navigate through streams of obstacles that move or appear. By tapping (or clicking), they trigger a swap of positions, letting them jump to where the echo is, bypassing obstacles that would have blocked the original. Then the echo rewinds and resumes following.

## Hazards / Obstacles:
Explain obstacles: static spikes that kill the dot; moving barriers that only affect the dot; zone fields that only affect the echo. Some obstacles are only dangerous to the real dot (e.g., electric fields) while the echo passes through harmless. Others are only dangerous to the echo (e.g., ghostly walls). Also moving walls require precise timing.

## Score:
Score increments for each successful swap, or for each obstacle avoided, or for distance traveled measured in segments. Additionally maybe collect items.

But we must not mention numbers; just discrete events: "performing a swap that avoids an obstacle", "collecting a glowing orb", "surviving a wave of hazards".

## Win and lose:
Win condition: survive a certain number of swaps or reach a target zone. Lose condition: dot collides with a hazard that kills it, or the echo triggers a hazard when swapping, causing death.

On win or lose, display message.

## Feel:
Vibe: frantic yet elegant; neon aesthetic with glowing trail; sound: subtle synth beeps.

## Why it's fun:
Single moment: swapping positions with the echo to slip past an impossible barrier, causing delight / surprise.

Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. - canvas: appears blank (only one color) after watch window — game may not be drawing

This is fix iteration 3 of 6. After your edits, the page will be reloaded and re-validated. If errors persist after 6 iterations, this game is abandoned.

Common interpretations:
- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

**Use your write/edit tool to apply the fixes.** Do NOT respond with code in chat — the files on disk are what gets validated.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called write/edit and that the files on disk now reflect your fixes.

prompt (13,941 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Now newString:

```
    // === LOOP / UPDATE ======================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // === UPDATE =============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      // Apply acceleration based on input keys
      const ACCEL = 600; // px/s²
      let ax = 0, ay = 0;
      if (keys.has("ArrowLeft") || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A")) ax -= 1;
      if (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D")) ax += 1;
      if (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W")) ay -= 1;
      if (keys.has("ArrowDown") || keys.has("s") || keys.has("S")) ay += 1;
      // Normalize diagonal acceleration if needed (optional)
      if (ax !== 0 && ay !== 0) {
        const norm = Math.sqrt(2);
        ax /= norm;
        ay /= norm;
      }
      // Accelerate
      player.vx += ax * ACCEL * dt;
      player.vy += ay * ACCEL * dt;
      // Apply friction
      const FRICTION = 0.9;
      player.vx *= FRICTION;
      player.vy *= FRICTION;
      // Cap speed
      const speed = Math.hypot(player.vx, player.vy);
      if (speed > MAX_SPEED) {
        const scale = MAX_SPEED / speed;
        player.vx *= scale;
        player.vy *= scale;
      }
      // Update position
      player.x += player.vx * dt;
      player.y += player.vy * dt;
      // Clamp to canvas bounds
      if (typeof canvas !== "undefined") {
        player.x = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.width - player.radius, player.x));
        player.y = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.height - player.radius, player.y));
      }
    }

    // === RENDER =============================================================
    function render() {
      // Placeholder render: clear canvas
      if (typeof ctx !== "undefined") {
        ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      }
    }

    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") {
        reset();
        return;
      }
      keys.add(ev.key);
    });
    document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
      keys.delete(ev.key);
    });
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");
    if (restartBtn) {
      restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);
    }
```

// === UPDATE =============================================================

function update(dt) {
  // Hazard spawning
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = 1.0;
  }
  // Update player movement placeholder (skip now)
}

// Helper spawnHazard
function spawnHazard() {
  const minDist = Math.max(SAFE_RADIUS, 200 * 3);
  let x, y;
  // generate random positions until far enough.
  do {
    x = Math.random() * canvas.width;
    y = Math.random() * canvas.height;
  } while (Math.hypot(x - player.x, y - player.y) < minDist);
  hazards.push({ x, y, radius: 8 });
}

// === RENDER =============================================================

function render() {
  ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  // draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252";
  for (const h of hazards) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, h.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();
  }
}

We also need to declare spawnTimer initial value. Could be part of reset: set spawnTimer = 1.0. Add variable declaration near other state placeholders: let spawnTimer = 0; (or let spawnTimer;). Let's add after keys: let spawnTimer = 0;

In reset, set spawnTimer = 1.0; for initial spawn after 1 sec.

Now adjust startLoop tick to call update and render:

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) { console.error("loop error", err); }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

 // Additional state placeholders

  // Placeholder reset: reinitialize state

// === LOOP / UPDATE ======================================================
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) { console.error("loop error", err); }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  // Placeholder update logic
}

// === RENDER ==============================================================
function render() {
  // Clear canvas
  ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // Draw placeholder player
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
}

// === BOOT ===============================================================
reset();

Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Echo Swap
Concept: The dot leaves a delayed echo that follows its exact path with a short lag; tapping swaps the positions of the dot and its echo, letting you bypass obstacles that only affect one instance.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path.
